## 9. Architecture and code review, 2026-09-12

The author's request for a comprehensive review adds a findings record to
this root before opening focused research children. The review covers the
engine, compiler, HTTP/CLI host, both client tiers and stores, React arms,
reference apps, simulator, fixtures, witnesses and release tooling at
Snapback 4 tree `cf0a13dca32b3e1721d1d947a3f82c262a0807f0`
(repository commit `d6c803878a5432cce5f045777786a049972d3d70`, version
0.0.16). It does not change the bakeoff decisions or accept new designs.

The prescribed suite passes, but additional probes reproduce
schema-admission and projection failures, lost or regressed replica state,
incorrect predictions, stale observations, compiler semantic errors and
request-worker starvation. Passing the existing suite does not establish
the missing properties. The author's updated scope reserves security holes
for another model; this record covers architecture, correctness,
performance and ergonomics and makes no security-review claim.

Four questions need design work beyond an isolated patch:

- [LLP 3000.000](./3000.000-atomic-schema-and-release-activation.research.md):
  the atomic unit and owner of schema/release activation.
- [LLP 3000.001](./3000.001-durable-replica-and-write-lifecycle.research.md):
  confirmed replica data, speculative writes and durable outcomes.
- [LLP 3000.002](./3000.002-end-to-end-resource-bounds.research.md):
  physical resource bounds across requests and replication.
- [LLP 3000.003](./3000.003-one-semantics-and-credible-verification.research.md):
  one semantic contract across Rust, TypeScript and verification hosts.

The [detailed review and 37-ticket index](./reviews/3000-architecture-code-review-20260912.md)
record evidence, alternatives and acceptance examples. All four children are
Draft Research. They remain review findings and proposals; the core owner
retains implementation custody under §1 decision 4.

The author subsequently requested implementation of the 37 concrete tickets.
All 37 are now closed; the [correction qualification record](./reviews/3000-review-corrections-20260913.md)
contains the executed checks, independent reviews and remaining research scope.
Their resolution records and the children's `Implementation follow-up`
sections describe the delivered boundaries: transactional semantic activation,
confirmed-base replay and atomic snapshot ownership, bounded admission/output,
and compiler/client parity corrections. Integration preserves the concurrently
landed native Expo module and ordered-merge performance work. The four children
remain Draft Research for their explicitly outstanding choices; the ticket
fixes do not claim that every architecture question has been settled.

The separate [security review](./reviews/3000-security-review-20260913.md)
opened twelve findings across the HTTP, identity, storage, synchronization and
asset boundaries. Its
[remediation record](./reviews/3000-security-remediation-20260913.md) closes
those findings and three overlapping comprehensive-review findings. The four
security decisions in §7 state the lasting contract; individual tickets retain
the counterexamples, acceptance evidence and limits.

## How to orient

Read this document, then LLP 2000.000 for the argument and its evidence,
then `snapback4/AGENTS.md` once it exists, then the journal. For the
sibling's position read LLP 2000; for the incumbent, LLP 1000; for the
history both inherit, LLP 0329 and its family.

## Non-claims

- This document accepts nothing in LLP 2000.000 on the author's behalf; it
  is the brief, and only running code binds.
- It changes no Snapback 2 or Snapback 3 status, scope, ceiling or gate, and
  edits neither rules file.
- It decides no promotion; the comparison and its ruling are the author's.
- It claims no result; every number above is a threshold or a plan.

## 2026-09-23 — Clique 0.3 follow-up implementation commission

Charlie commissioned Astra max or xhigh to implement the accepted
[modeling plan](./3000.011.000.001-modeling-after-clique-030.plan.md) and
[scale plan](./3000.011.001.001-scale-after-clique-030.plan.md). Their earlier
sanity review found no material concerns. Implementation preserves each plan’s
measurement-first sequence and stopping conditions. Historical research links
3000.000 and 3000.001 leave the current overlay to make room; the documents
remain. Package publication and mutation of the frozen benchmark are outside
this commission.

## 2026-09-27 — Global search beyond one read

Lane GS reproduced the global-search boundary and stopped at a design handoff.
The supervisor narrowed 0.4.6 to compact, bounded one-read membership search;
[LLP 3000.024](./3000.024-global-search-beyond-one-read.rfc.md) records the
Decision, implementation, measurements and explicit grouped-search refusal
beyond its envelope. The retained-session and stateless multi-pass contracts
remain future RFC work. Budgets, disclosure rules and the no-query-cache
exclusion are unchanged; round 1's report records qualification failures.

 succeeded in 0ms:
//! Atomic adoption of the maintained-row address function in the evaluation
//! adapter. The marker and generation publish in the same writer transaction
//! as the target rebuild, so no writer can observe mixed legacy/digest ids.
use super::*;

pub(super) fn install(
    tx: &store::Tx<'_>,
    schema: &Schema,
    creating: bool,
    generation: u64,
) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
    let current = tx.query(
        "SELECT maintained_identity_version FROM _backend WHERE singleton",
        &[],
    )?[0].get::<_, Option<&str>>(0).map(str::to_owned);
    if current.as_deref() == Some(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION) {
        return Ok(generation);
    }

    let aggregates: Vec<_> = schema.maintains.iter()
        .filter(|maintain| maintain.row_key.is_none())
        .cloned()
        .collect();
    if !creating && !aggregates.is_empty() {
        let mut runner = Runner::new(
            tx,
            schema,
            Context::system().at(crate::now_ms()),
            Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort),
            tx.next_seq()?,
        );
        runner.event_free = true;
        runner.rewrite_legacy_maintained_references(&aggregates, None, None)?;
        for maintain in &aggregates {
            runner.rebuild_maintain(maintain)?;
        }
        // The narrow maintained-column shape admitted by this adapter keeps
        // its private target out of the public rows but still shares the same
        // identity upgrade. Rebuild every target before publishing the marker,
        // then restore its authoritative value into each live parent.
        // @ref LLP 3000.007.001#3.11 — activation is rebuild, then mirror, before the generation barrier.
        for maintain in aggregates.iter().filter(|maintain| maintain.private.is_some()) {
            runner.mirror_column_pass(maintain)?;
        }
    }

    let generation = if creating || aggregates.is_empty() {
        generation
    } else {
        generation.checked_add(1)
            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Generation("generation overflow".into()))?
    };
    tx.execute(
        "UPDATE _backend SET maintained_identity_version=$1, generation=$2 WHERE singleton",
        &[&crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION, &store::signed(generation)?],
    )?;
    Ok(generation)
}

Only a timeline's owned tables are refused now; its mapped tables are ordinary parents and sources, shown by a differential test (§3.1, §12)); 2026-09-22 (revision 7: lane F3b's counterexample and lane F3a's review. The stream re-reads current row images into historical commits, so no write-time check can keep an old commit deliverable. A 146 KB, 1,024-row commit grew to 18.5 MB after 1,021 later one-row updates that each fit. Deliverability is restated: each form bounds its own derived rows, and a lagging client recovers through the existing snapshot restart at limit 1, tested end to end. Owned timeline rows take digest identities derived from their natural keys, never caller-supplied ids, which closes an existence probe on private inboxes; §3.3); 2026-09-22 (revision 6: lane F3a's second counterexample. Lazy author registration moves a write into the first post and breaks "never refuse what the recipe admits" at the write ceiling. Registration is now eager: on profile insert after activation, and once at activation for existing profiles, bounded by the author cap); 2026-09-22 (revision 5: lane F3a's counterexample. The recipe writes a duplicate follow edge, and F3 drops it, so the maximum-backfill promotion uses 1,023 writes against the recipe's 1,024. The recipe-trace oracle allows F3 to admit what the recipe refuses only for that duplicate write, never the reverse); 2026-09-22 (revision 4, after the focused F3 review, READY WITH CHANGES. Inputs are transaction-visible rows applied as actual transitions. F3 gains the removal flag's transition rules, correct attribution of fresh event identity (N1), lazy author registration, a trusted internal write path, the disclosure and device-execution rules, and restoration and import rules. The follow row is corrected to 1,000 source rows read, a 865-event window and the 159 allowance including the application's edge (N2). Deliverability covers composed commits, and F3's oracle is recipe-trace equivalence. Dispositions are in §12); 2026-09-22 (revision 3, after the Astra max delta review found revision 2 still NOT READY on D1 and D4. F2 gains a full contract: preserved recorded paths, activation-time capture, live resolution, field ownership including `Engine::load`, nullability, and event-free activation. F3 names its durable state, gains a declared removal signal and an empty-table first scope, and corrects its history window and bounds. §5's delivery cases are corrected. The §11 decisions were taken under the author's delegation as LLP 3000.007 rulings 45–51. Dispositions are in §12); 2026-09-22 (revision 2, after the [Astra max review](./reviews/3000.012-application-still-owns-derived-data.astra.md) found revision 1 NOT READY: F2 becomes engine-recorded attribution over public targets; F3 states the recipe's answer contract and operation matrix within its supported shape; the substrate test becomes shared interfaces with per-form lifecycle policies; MC1 is framed as a gate revision; the acceptance counters are corrected; dispositions in §12); 2026-09-22 (initial)

@@ -170,6 +170,21 @@ target stays opaque, preserving the existing dangling-reference contract.
 Removing the aggregate itself retires its derived target rows; a dormant
 reference to that retired target receives no map and keeps the ordinary
 restore refusal against acquiring a newly created parent lifetime.
+
+Persisted `sequence by` custody is part of the same identity migration even
+when no allocating row, source row, or aggregate target row survives. Adoption
+decodes canonical legacy counter ids with the predecessor aggregate's typed
+group definition, recursively settles any aggregate-reference components, and
+re-keys `_sequences(parent, id, value)` through the resulting target-specific
+map. If legacy and digest keys both exist, their maximum high-water value wins;
+the legacy key is removed and an exhausted counter remains exhausted. Counters
+for other parent tables and legacy strings that do not decode as a canonical
+group tuple stay opaque. A predecessor aggregate removed by the candidate is
+normalized before the current identity marker publishes, so later
+reintroduction cannot revive legacy custody under that marker. Counter rewrites
+and their rollback share the adoption transaction with row-reference rewrites,
+target rebuilds, marker and generation.
+
 Reference rewrites, index updates, target rebuilds, marker and generation share
 the same transaction. A legacy target address is itself the predecessor's
 canonical logical group tuple. Adoption decodes it using the predecessor

+#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
+type LegacyIdentities = Vec<(String, Vec<(String, Vec<(crate::schema::ColumnType, Value)>)>)>;
+
+#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
+type LegacyIdentityMap = BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, Vec<(crate::schema::ColumnType, Value)>>>;
+
+#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
+fn collect_sequence_identity_dependencies(
+    kind: &crate::schema::ColumnType,
+    value: &Value,
+    definitions: &BTreeMap<String, Vec<crate::schema::ColumnType>>,
+    identities: &mut LegacyIdentityMap,
+) {
+    match (kind, value) {
+        (crate::schema::ColumnType::Ref { table }, Value::Id(old)) => {
+            let Some(kinds) = definitions.get(table) else { return };
+            if identities.get(table).is_some_and(|ids| ids.contains_key(old)) { return; }
+            let Some(values) = crate::json_codec::from_text::<Vec<Value>>(old).ok()
+                .filter(|values| values.len() == kinds.len()) else { return };
+            let key: Vec<_> = kinds.iter().cloned().zip(values).collect();
+            identities.entry(table.clone()).or_default().insert(old.clone(), key.clone());
+            for (kind, value) in &key {
+                collect_sequence_identity_dependencies(kind, value, definitions, identities);
+            }
+        }
+        (crate::schema::ColumnType::Optional(inner), value) =>
+            collect_sequence_identity_dependencies(inner, value, definitions, identities),
+        (crate::schema::ColumnType::List { of, .. } | crate::schema::ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, Value::Array(values)) =>
+            for value in values { collect_sequence_identity_dependencies(of, value, definitions, identities); },
+        _ => {}
+    }
+}
+
+#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
+fn insert_sequence_identity(
+    target: &str,
+    old: String,
+    key: Vec<(crate::schema::ColumnType, Value)>,
+    definitions: &BTreeMap<String, Vec<crate::schema::ColumnType>>,
+    identities: &mut LegacyIdentityMap,
+) {
+    if identities.get(target).is_some_and(|ids| ids.contains_key(&old)) { return; }
+    identities.entry(target.to_owned()).or_default().insert(old, key.clone());
+    for (kind, value) in &key {
+        collect_sequence_identity_dependencies(kind, value, definitions, identities);
+    }
+}
+
+#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
+fn settle_legacy_maintained_ids(
+    legacy: &LegacyIdentities,
+    base: &BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, String>>,
+) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, String>>, RuntimeError> {
+    let mut ids = base.clone();
+    let identity_count = legacy.iter().map(|(_, rows)| rows.len()).sum::<usize>();
+    for _ in 0..=identity_count.saturating_add(1) {
+        let mut next_ids = base.clone();
+        for (target, rows) in legacy {
+            let target_ids = next_ids.entry(target.clone()).or_default();
+            for (old, key) in rows {
+                let key: Vec<_> = key.iter().map(|(kind, value)| {
+                    let mut value = value.clone();
+                    rewrite_legacy_maintained_reference(kind, &mut value, &ids);
+                    value
+                }).collect();
+                let next = maintained_id(&key);
+                if old != &next { target_ids.insert(old.clone(), next); }
+            }
+        }
+        if next_ids == ids { return Ok(next_ids); }
+        ids = next_ids;
+    }
+    Err(RuntimeError::Schema("legacy aggregate identity references form a cycle".into()))
+}
+

+    fn rewrite_sequence_ids(&self, ids: &BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, String>>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
+        let mut rewritten = 0u64;
+        for (parent, parent_ids) in ids.iter().filter(|(_, ids)| !ids.is_empty()) {

+            let mut merged = BTreeMap::<String, i64>::new();

+                parent_rewritten = parent_rewritten.saturating_add(u64::from(next != old));

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store.rs
index 1ddd3ba416..bab5e0db9c 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store.rs
@@ -1083,6 +1083,34 @@ impl crate::rows::Rows for Tx<'_> {
         self.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _sequences VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3) ON CONFLICT(parent, id) DO UPDATE SET value = excluded.value", params![parent, id, value])?;
         Ok(())
     }
+    fn sequence_ids(&self, parent: &str) -> Result<Vec<String>, RuntimeError> {
+        if !self.has_sequence_table()? { return Ok(Vec::new()); }
+        Ok(self.conn().prepare_cached("SELECT id FROM _sequences WHERE parent=?1 ORDER BY id")?
+            .query_map([parent], |row| row.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?)
+    }
+    fn rewrite_sequence_ids(&self, ids: &BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, String>>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
+        if !self.has_sequence_table()? { return Ok(0); }
+        let mut rewritten = 0u64;
+        for (parent, parent_ids) in ids.iter().filter(|(_, ids)| !ids.is_empty()) {
+            let rows: Vec<(String, i64)> = self.conn().prepare_cached(
+                "SELECT id,value FROM _sequences WHERE parent=?1 ORDER BY id"
+            )?.query_map([parent], |row| Ok((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)))?
+                .collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
+            let mut merged = BTreeMap::<String, i64>::new();
+            let mut parent_rewritten = 0u64;
+            for (old, value) in rows {
+                let next = parent_ids.get(&old).cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| old.clone());
+                parent_rewritten = parent_rewritten.saturating_add(u64::from(next != old));
+                merged.entry(next).and_modify(|current| *current = (*current).max(value)).or_insert(value);
+            }
+            if parent_rewritten == 0 || merged.is_empty() { continue; }
+            self.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _sequences WHERE parent=?1", [parent])?;
+            let mut insert = self.conn().prepare_cached("INSERT INTO _sequences(parent,id,value) VALUES (?1,?2,?3)")?;
+            for (id, value) in merged { insert.execute(params![parent, id, value])?; }
+            rewritten = rewritten.saturating_add(parent_rewritten);
+        }
+        Ok(rewritten)
+    }

+#[test]
+fn identity_adoption_rekeys_sequence_history_without_reusing_deleted_ordinals() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table entries:\n  bucket: int\n  public\nview totals: count entries by .bucket\n  public\ntable sequenced:\n  total: totals\n  ordinal: sequence by total\n  unique total, ordinal\n  public\n";
+    let mut e = open(SOURCE);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("e7".into())), ("bucket", Value::Int(7))])),
+        ("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("e9".into())), ("bucket", Value::Int(9))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let digest7 = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let digest9 = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(9)]);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("sequenced".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("gone".into())), ("total", Value::Id(digest7.clone()))])),
+        ("sequenced".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("kept".into())), ("total", Value::Id(digest7.clone()))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    e.seed_delete("sequenced", "gone").unwrap();
+
+    let legacy7 = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let legacy9 = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(9)]);
+    let schema = e.schema().clone();
+    let target = schema.tables["totals"].clone();
+    let entries = schema.tables["entries"].clone();
+    let sequenced = schema.tables["sequenced"].clone();
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    let mut row = tx.get("totals", &digest7).unwrap().unwrap();
+    tx.delete("totals", &target, &row).unwrap();
+    if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row {
+        fields.insert("id".into(), Value::Id(legacy7.clone()));
+    }
+    tx.put("totals", &target, &row, None).unwrap();
+    tx.delete("totals", &target, &tx.get("totals", &digest9).unwrap().unwrap()).unwrap();
+    tx.delete("entries", &entries, &tx.get("entries", "e9").unwrap().unwrap()).unwrap();
+    let old = tx.get("sequenced", "kept").unwrap().unwrap();
+    let mut next = old.clone();
+    if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut next {
+        fields.insert("total".into(), Value::Id(legacy7.clone()));
+    }
+    tx.put("sequenced", &sequenced, &next, Some(&old)).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &legacy7, 7).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &digest7, 4).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &legacy9, crate::value::I53_MAX).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &digest9, 12).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("ordinary", &legacy7, 11).unwrap();
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+
+    let next = compile(SOURCE);
+    e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &legacy7).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &legacy9).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest7).unwrap(), 7, "the deleted allocating row and lower destination counter cannot lower the high-water mark");
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest9).unwrap(), crate::value::I53_MAX);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("ordinary", &legacy7).unwrap(), 11, "translation is scoped by parent table and id");
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    let kept = e.store().rows("sequenced").unwrap().pop().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(kept.field("total").unwrap(), &Value::Id(digest7.clone()));
+    assert_eq!(kept.field("ordinal").unwrap(), &Value::Int(2));
+
+    e.seed_at([("sequenced".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("next".into())), ("total", Value::Id(digest7))]))], 2).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("sequenced").unwrap().into_iter().find(|row| row.field("id") == Ok(&Value::Id("next".into()))).unwrap().field("ordinal"), Ok(&Value::Int(8)));
+    let error = e.seed_at([("sequenced".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("full".into())), ("total", Value::Id(digest9.clone()))]))], 2).unwrap_err();
+    assert_eq!(error.refusal().code, "E_SEQUENCE_EXHAUSTED");
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest9).unwrap(), crate::value::I53_MAX, "overflow refusal must not change the migrated counter");
+    tx.rollback().unwrap();
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn removing_an_aggregate_normalizes_retained_sequence_custody_before_marking_the_store() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table entries:\n  bucket: int\n  public\nview totals: count entries by .bucket\n  public\ntable sequenced:\n  total: totals\n  ordinal: sequence by total\n  unique total, ordinal\n  public\n";
+    const WITHOUT_AGGREGATE: &str = "table entries:\n  bucket: int\n  public\n";
+    let mut e = open(SOURCE);
+    e.seed_at([("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("entry".into())), ("bucket", Value::Int(3))]))], 0).unwrap();
+    let digest = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(3)]);
+    let legacy = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(3)]);
+    let target = e.schema().tables["totals"].clone();
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    let mut row = tx.get("totals", &digest).unwrap().unwrap();
+    tx.delete("totals", &target, &row).unwrap();
+    if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row {
+        fields.insert("id".into(), Value::Id(legacy.clone()));
+    }
+    tx.put("totals", &target, &row, None).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &legacy, 5).unwrap();
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+
+    let removed = compile(WITHOUT_AGGREGATE);
+    e.adopt_at(removed.schema, removed.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &legacy).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest).unwrap(), 5);
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(e.store().meta(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META).unwrap().as_deref(), Some(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION));
+
+    let restored = compile(SOURCE);
+    e.adopt_at(restored.schema, restored.programs, 2).unwrap();
+    e.seed_at([("sequenced".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("next".into())), ("total", Value::Id(digest))]))], 3).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("sequenced").unwrap()[0].field("ordinal"), Ok(&Value::Int(6)));
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn counter_only_aggregate_reference_chains_rekey_to_the_final_digest() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table aEntries:\n  key: int\n  public\nview aTotals: count aEntries by .key\n  public\ntable bEntries:\n  parent: aTotals\n  public\nview bTotals: count bEntries by .parent\n  public\ntable sequenced:\n  parent: bTotals\n  ordinal: sequence by parent\n  public\n";
+    let mut e = open(SOURCE);
+    let legacy_a = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(5)]);
+    let legacy_b = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Id(legacy_a.clone())]);
+    let digest_a = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(5)]);
+    let digest_b = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Id(digest_a)]);
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("bTotals", &legacy_b, 9).unwrap();
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+
+    let next = compile(SOURCE);
+    e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("bTotals", &legacy_b).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("bTotals", &digest_b).unwrap(), 9, "the absent referenced aggregate identity must settle before its dependent counter key");
+    tx.rollback().unwrap();
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn sequence_identity_rekey_rolls_back_with_the_adoption_writer() {
+    let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
+    let legacy = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let digest = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &legacy, 11).unwrap();
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+
+    let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
+    tx.rewrite_sequence_ids(&BTreeMap::from([(
+        "totals".into(),
+        BTreeMap::from([(legacy.clone(), digest.clone())]),
+    )])).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &legacy).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest).unwrap(), 11);
+    tx.rollback().unwrap();
+
+    let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &legacy).unwrap(), 11);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest).unwrap(), 0);
+    tx.rollback().unwrap();
+}
+

 fn changed_filter_counts_cleared_retained_view_rows() {
     rebuild_groups(&GROUPS.replace("count entries by", "count entries where .n < 0 by"), 0);

+mutation removeEntry(id: entries):

+#[test]
+#[ignore = "requires frozen 0.4.28 binary via SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN"]
+fn a_0_4_28_store_preserves_counter_only_sequence_history() {
+    let baseline = PathBuf::from(
+        std::env::var_os("SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN")
+            .expect("set SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN to the frozen 0.4.28 snapback4 binary"),
+    );
+    assert!(baseline.is_file(), "SNAPBACK4_BASELINE_BIN is not a file: {}", baseline.display());
+    let dir = Temp::new(SEQUENCE_SOURCE);
+    let legacy = snapback4_core::value::canonical_json(&[snapback4_core::Value::Int(7)]);
+    let run = |binary: &Path, operation: &str, args: serde_json::Value, now: &str| {
+        let output = Command::new(binary).current_dir(&dir.0)
+            .args(["run", operation, &args.to_string(), "--as", "alice", "--now", now, "--json"])
+            .output().unwrap();
+        assert!(output.status.success(), "{operation}: stdout={} stderr={}",
+            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout), String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr));
+        output
+    };
+    run(&baseline, "addEntry", serde_json::json!({"id":"entry-old","bucket":7}), "1");
+    for n in 1..=3 {
+        run(&baseline, "addOrder", serde_json::json!({"id":format!("order-{n}"),"total":legacy}), "1");
+    }
+    for n in 1..=3 {
+        run(&baseline, "removeOrder", serde_json::json!({"id":format!("order-{n}")}), "1");
+    }
+    run(&baseline, "removeEntry", serde_json::json!({"id":"entry-old"}), "1");
+
+    let store = dir.0.join(".snapback4/store.sqlite");
+    let conn = Connection::open(&store).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(conn.query_row("SELECT value FROM _sequences WHERE parent='totals' AND id=?1", [&legacy], |row| row.get::<_, i64>(0)).unwrap(), 3);
+    assert_eq!(conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM r JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=r.t WHERE c.name IN ('entries','totals','ordered')", [], |row| row.get::<_, i64>(0)).unwrap(), 0);
+    drop(conn);
+
+    let current = Path::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_snapback4"));
+    run(current, "addEntry", serde_json::json!({"id":"entry-new","bucket":7}), "2");
+    let digest = snapback4_core::schema::maintained_id(&[snapback4_core::Value::Int(7)]);
+    run(current, "addOrder", serde_json::json!({"id":"order-4","total":digest}), "2");
+
+    let conn = Connection::open(&store).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM _sequences WHERE parent='totals' AND id=?1", [&legacy], |row| row.get::<_, i64>(0)).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(conn.query_row("SELECT value FROM _sequences WHERE parent='totals' AND id=?1", [&digest], |row| row.get::<_, i64>(0)).unwrap(), 4);
+    let data: String = conn.query_row(
+        "SELECT r.data FROM r JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=r.t WHERE c.name='ordered' AND r.id='order-4'",
+        [], |row| row.get(0),
+    ).unwrap();
+    let row: snapback4_core::Value = serde_json::from_str(&data).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(row.field("ordinal").unwrap(), &snapback4_core::Value::Int(4));
+}

+/-! Persisted `sequence by` counters are server custody keyed by the parent
+table and physical parent id. They outlive allocating rows. This small model
+does not model SQL or the device protocol; it states the adoption invariant
+for one target-specific legacy-to-digest key. -/
+
+def sequenceHighWater (id : List Nat) (counters : List (List Nat × Nat)) : Nat :=
+  counters.foldl (fun high counter =>
+    if counter.1 = id then max high counter.2 else high) 0
+
+/-- If a partially upgraded store already has both keys, adoption merges them
+at their maximum. No child-row population participates in this value. -/
+def adoptedSequenceHighWater (legacy digest : List Nat)
+    (counters : List (List Nat × Nat)) : Nat :=
+  max (sequenceHighWater legacy counters) (sequenceHighWater digest counters)
+
+theorem adoptedSequence_preserves_legacy (legacy digest counters) :
+    sequenceHighWater legacy counters ≤ adoptedSequenceHighWater legacy digest counters :=
+  Nat.le_max_left _ _
+
+theorem adoptedSequence_preserves_digest (legacy digest counters) :
+    sequenceHighWater digest counters ≤ adoptedSequenceHighWater legacy digest counters :=
+  Nat.le_max_right _ _
+
+example : adoptedSequenceHighWater [91, 55, 93] (maintainedIdV1 [.int 7])
+    [([91, 55, 93], 9), (maintainedIdV1 [.int 7], 4)] = 9 := by decide
+
 example : (maintainedIdV1 []).length = 64 := maintainedIdV1_length []
 example : (maintainedIdV1 [.id (List.replicate 64 97), .text (List.replicate 64 120)]).length = 64 :=
   maintainedIdV1_length _

    27	        crate::schema::ColumnType::Optional(inner)

    73	fn collect_sequence_identity_dependencies(

   113	#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   114	fn settle_legacy_maintained_ids(
   115	    legacy: &LegacyIdentities,
   116	    base: &BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, String>>,
   117	) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, String>>, RuntimeError> {
   118	    let mut ids = base.clone();
   119	    let identity_count = legacy.iter().map(|(_, rows)| rows.len()).sum::<usize>();
   120	    for _ in 0..=identity_count.saturating_add(1) {
   121	        let mut next_ids = base.clone();
   122	        for (target, rows) in legacy {
   123	            let target_ids = next_ids.entry(target.clone()).or_default();
   124	            for (old, key) in rows {
   125	                let key: Vec<_> = key.iter().map(|(kind, value)| {
   126	                    let mut value = value.clone();
   127	                    rewrite_legacy_maintained_reference(kind, &mut value, &ids);
   128	                    value
   129	                }).collect();
   130	                let next = maintained_id(&key);
   131	                if old != &next { target_ids.insert(old.clone(), next); }
   132	            }
   133	        }
   134	        if next_ids == ids { return Ok(next_ids); }
   135	        ids = next_ids;
   136	    }
   137	    Err(RuntimeError::Schema("legacy aggregate identity references form a cycle".into()))
   138	}

   140	impl Runner<'_> {
   141	    // @ref LLP 3000.022#14.3 — no predicted delta enters receipt-derived state.
   142	    pub(super) fn defer_receipt_maintain(&self, m: &Maintain) -> bool {
   143	        self.device && self.schema.receipt_dependencies.contains_key(&m.target)
   144	    }
   145	
   146	    // Cache ordinary group keys once per target/query. Predicates and receipt
   147	    // fields are never evaluated; unknown/computed keys conservatively freeze
   148	    // the target. An unpinned read is incomplete if any group is dirty, while
   149	    // each returned row still decides independently whether to recount.
   150	    #[inline(never)]
   151	    pub(super) fn receipt_maintain_pending(&mut self, m: &Maintain, group: Option<&[Value]>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
   152	        if !self.defer_receipt_maintain(m) { return Ok(false); }
   153	        if !self.receipt_pending_groups.contains_key(&m.target) {
   154	            let fields = if let Some(k) = &m.row_key { vec![k.source_key.clone()] }
   155	                else { m.by.iter().map(|f| m.aliases.get(f).unwrap_or(f).clone()).collect() };
   156	            let mut groups = self.receipt_overlay_groups(&m.source, &fields, !m.computed.is_empty())?;
   157	            if let Some(k) = &m.row_key {
   158	                let member = self.receipt_overlay_groups(&k.table, std::slice::from_ref(&k.member_key), false)?;
   159	                groups = groups.zip(member).map(|(mut source, member)| { source.extend(member); source });
   160	            }
   161	            self.receipt_pending_groups.insert(m.target.clone(), groups);
   162	        }
   163	        let dirty = self.receipt_pending_groups[&m.target].as_ref().is_none_or(|keys|
   164	            group.map_or(!keys.is_empty(), |group| keys.iter().any(|key| key == group)));
   165	        if dirty {
   166	            self.value_source_incomplete = true;
   167	            self.components.note(|frame| frame.pending = true);
   168	        }
   169	        Ok(dirty)
   170	    }
   171	
   172	    fn receipt_overlay_groups(&self, table: &str, fields: &[String], computed: bool) -> Result<Option<Vec<Vec<Value>>>, RuntimeError> {
   173	        if !self.tx.has_provisional(table)? { return Ok(Some(Vec::new())); }
   174	        if computed || fields.iter().any(|f| self.schema.receipt_dependencies.get(table).is_some_and(|owned| owned.contains(f))) {
   175	            return Ok(None);
   176	        }
   177	        self.tx.provisional_groups(table, fields)
   178	    }
   179	
   180	    // Recover a read's parent/group from the pinned index. A member point uses
   181	    // one ordinary-key lookup; a scan without the parent stays conservative.
   182	    pub(super) fn receipt_read_group(&mut self, m: &Maintain, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> Result<Option<Vec<Value>>, RuntimeError> {
   183	        if !self.defer_receipt_maintain(m) { return Ok(None); }
   184	        let columns = self.schema.tables[&m.target].indexes[index].columns();
   185	        let pinned = |field: &str| columns.iter().position(|c| c == field).and_then(|i| key.get(i)).cloned();
   186	        if let Some(k) = &m.row_key {
   187	            if let Some(parent) = pinned(&k.member_key) { return Ok(Some(vec![parent])); }
   188	            if let Some(id) = pinned("member").or_else(|| pinned("id")) {
   189	                let row = self.metered_scan(|r, scope| r.tx.get(scope, &k.table, id.as_str()?))?;
   190	                return Ok(row.and_then(|row| row.field(&k.member_key).ok().cloned()).map(|v| vec![v]));
   191	            }
   192	            return Ok(None);
   193	        }
   194	        Ok(m.by.iter().map(|f| pinned(f)).collect())
   195	    }

   197	    pub(super) fn maintain(&mut self, source: &str, old: Option<&Value>, new: Option<&Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   198	        self.receipt_pending_groups.clear();
   199	        // Inserts ordinarily charge their actual aggregate work. A deferred
   200	        // predicate cannot select that work on a device, so use the same public
   201	        // worst-case reservation as update/delete before skipping it. Already
   202	        // prepaid writes must not pay twice.
   203	        if self.device && !self.maintain_prepaid && self.schema.maintains.iter().any(|m|
   204	            m.source == source && m.private.is_none() && m.row_key.is_none() && (self.schema.receipt_dependencies.contains_key(&m.target) || self.schema.tables[&m.target].sync.is_none())) {
   205	            self.reserve_maintains(source, &self.schema.tables[source])?;
   206	        }
   207	        self.maintain_row_views(source, old, new)?;
   208	        let maintains = self.schema.maintains.iter().filter(|maintain| maintain.source == source && maintain.row_key.is_none());
   209	        for maintain in maintains {
   210	            if self.defer_receipt_maintain(maintain) || (self.device && self.schema.tables[&maintain.target].sync.is_none()) { continue; }
   211	            let source_bytes = super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[&maintain.source]);
   212	            let target = &self.schema.tables[&maintain.target];
   213	            let bytes = source_bytes.saturating_mul(2).saturating_add(super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(target).saturating_mul(16 + 4 * target.indexes.len() as u64));
   214	            let prepaid = self.maintain_prepaid;
   215	            if maintain.private.is_some() && self.column_prepaid { self.maintain_prepaid = true; }
   216	            let result = self.maintain_scratch(bytes, |runner| runner.maintain_one(maintain, old, new));
   217	            self.maintain_prepaid = prepaid;
   218	            result?;
   219	        }
   220	        Ok(())
   221	    }
   222	
   223	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9 — reserve interpreted images, both keys, targets and index buffers before allocation; release transient ownership on every exit.
   224	    pub(super) fn maintain_scratch<T>(&mut self, bytes: u64, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
   225	        if self.maintain_prepaid { return work(self); }
   226	        self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
   227	        let result = work(self);
   228	        self.meter.used.heap_bytes = self.meter.used.heap_bytes.saturating_sub(bytes);
   229	        result
   230	    }
   231	
   232	    fn maintain_one(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain, old: Option<&Value>, new: Option<&Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   233	        let table = self.schema.table(&maintain.source).expect("validated");
   234	        let mut changes: Vec<(Vec<Value>, i128)> = Vec::new();
   235	        for (image, direction) in [(old, -1), (new, 1)] {
   236	            let Some(image) = image else { continue };
   237	            self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::Steps, maintain.evaluation_steps())?;
   238	            let image = crate::schema::row_image(image, table);
   239	            if !maintain.selected(&image) { continue; }
   240	            let key = maintain.source_key(&image);
   241	            let change = delta(maintain, &image, direction)?;
   242	            if let Some(existing) = changes.iter_mut().find(|(k, _)| *k == key) { existing.1 += change; }
   243	            else { changes.push((key, change)); }
   244	        }
   245	        for (key, change) in changes {
   246	            if let Some(retiring) = self.retiring.as_mut() {
   247	                let entry = retiring.aggregates.entry((maintain.target.clone(), canonical_json(&key))).or_insert_with(|| (key, 0));
   248	                entry.1 = entry.1.checked_add(change).ok_or_else(|| aggregate_overflow(maintain))?;
   249	                continue;
   250	            }
   251	            if change != 0 || self.live_sum(maintain) {
   252	                self.apply_aggregate(maintain, key, change, None)?;
   253	            }
   254	        }
   255	        Ok(())
   256	    }
   257	
   258	    /// Apply a retirement's net aggregate changes, one per target row, in
   259	    /// key order. Only a total the commit really ends with can overflow.
   260	    pub(super) fn apply_retired_aggregates(&mut self, changes: BTreeMap<(String, String), (Vec<Value>, i128)>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   261	        let schema = self.schema;
   262	        for ((target, _), (key, change)) in changes {
   263	            let maintain = schema.maintain_for(&target).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing maintain for {target}")))?;
   264	            if change != 0 || self.live_sum(maintain) {
   265	                self.apply_aggregate(maintain, key, change, None)?;
   266	            }
   267	        }
   268	        Ok(())
   269	    }

   271	    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   272	    pub(crate) fn rebuild_maintain_reported(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain, report: &mut crate::store::adoption_report::AdoptionReport) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   273	        let started = std::time::Instant::now();
   274	        self.rebuilt_source_rows = 0;
   275	        self.rebuild_maintain(maintain)?;
   276	        let rows = self.rebuilt_source_rows;
   277	        report.views.push(crate::store::adoption_report::ViewBuild { name: maintain.label(), rows, elapsed: started.elapsed() });
   278	        Ok(())
   279	    }

   281	    /// Derive every legacy-to-digest aggregate id map to a fixed point, then
   282	    /// rewrite typed references before rebuilding the target rows. The fixed
   283	    /// point matters when one aggregate groups by a reference to another.
   284	    /// Every pass is inside the adoption writer transaction, so no reader can
   285	    /// observe a dangling intermediate reference.
   286	    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   287	    pub(crate) fn rewrite_legacy_maintained_references(&mut self, maintains: &[Maintain], previous: Option<&crate::schema::Schema>, activation_tx: Option<&crate::store::Tx<'_>>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
   288	        let mut legacy: LegacyIdentities = Vec::new();
   289	        let mut definitions = BTreeMap::<String, Vec<crate::schema::ColumnType>>::new();
   290	        for maintain in maintains.iter().filter(|maintain| maintain.private.is_none() && maintain.row_key.is_none()) {
   291	            let target = &self.schema.tables[&maintain.target];
   292	            definitions.insert(maintain.target.clone(), maintain.group_fields().into_iter()
   293	                .map(|field| target.columns[&field].clone()).collect());
   294	        }
   295	        if let Some(previous) = previous {
   296	            for maintain in previous.maintains.iter().filter(|maintain| maintain.private.is_none() && maintain.row_key.is_none()) {
   297	                let target = &previous.tables[&maintain.target];
   298	                definitions.insert(maintain.target.clone(), maintain.group_fields().into_iter()

   313	                .unwrap_or_else(|| &self.schema.tables[&maintain.target]);

   373	        // its parent key persists after target/source/child deletion, so its
   374	        // independently decoded legacy tuple joins the same fixed point.

   441	    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   442	    fn rebuild_maintain_inner(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   443	        if maintain.row_key.is_some() { return self.rebuild_row_view(maintain); }

   459	        let source = self.schema.table(&maintain.source).expect("validated");
   460	        self.meter.heap(super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(source).saturating_mul(256))?;

+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let digest7 = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let digest9 = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(9)]);
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("sequenced".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("gone".into())), ("total", Value::Id(digest7.clone()))])),
+        ("sequenced".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("kept".into())), ("total", Value::Id(digest7.clone()))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    e.seed_delete("sequenced", "gone").unwrap();

+    let legacy7 = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let legacy9 = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(9)]);

+    let entries = schema.tables["entries"].clone();

+    if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row {
+        fields.insert("id".into(), Value::Id(legacy7.clone()));
+    }
+    tx.put("totals", &target, &row, None).unwrap();
+    tx.delete("totals", &target, &tx.get("totals", &digest9).unwrap().unwrap()).unwrap();
+    tx.delete("entries", &entries, &tx.get("entries", "e9").unwrap().unwrap()).unwrap();
+    let old = tx.get("sequenced", "kept").unwrap().unwrap();
+    let mut next = old.clone();
+    if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut next {
+        fields.insert("total".into(), Value::Id(legacy7.clone()));

+    tx.put("sequenced", &sequenced, &next, Some(&old)).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &legacy7, 7).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &digest7, 4).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &legacy9, crate::value::I53_MAX).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &digest9, 12).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("ordinary", &legacy7, 11).unwrap();
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+
+    let next = compile(SOURCE);
+    e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &legacy7).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &legacy9).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest7).unwrap(), 7, "the deleted allocating row and lower destination counter cannot lower the high-water mark");
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest9).unwrap(), crate::value::I53_MAX);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("ordinary", &legacy7).unwrap(), 11, "translation is scoped by parent table and id");
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    let kept = e.store().rows("sequenced").unwrap().pop().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(kept.field("total").unwrap(), &Value::Id(digest7.clone()));
+    assert_eq!(kept.field("ordinal").unwrap(), &Value::Int(2));
+
+    e.seed_at([("sequenced".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("next".into())), ("total", Value::Id(digest7))]))], 2).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("sequenced").unwrap().into_iter().find(|row| row.field("id") == Ok(&Value::Id("next".into()))).unwrap().field("ordinal"), Ok(&Value::Int(8)));
+    let error = e.seed_at([("sequenced".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("full".into())), ("total", Value::Id(digest9.clone()))]))], 2).unwrap_err();
+    assert_eq!(error.refusal().code, "E_SEQUENCE_EXHAUSTED");
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest9).unwrap(), crate::value::I53_MAX, "overflow refusal must not change the migrated counter");
+    tx.rollback().unwrap();
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn removing_an_aggregate_normalizes_retained_sequence_custody_before_marking_the_store() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table entries:\n  bucket: int\n  public\nview totals: count entries by .bucket\n  public\ntable sequenced:\n  total: totals\n  ordinal: sequence by total\n  unique total, ordinal\n  public\n";
+    const WITHOUT_AGGREGATE: &str = "table entries:\n  bucket: int\n  public\n";
+    let mut e = open(SOURCE);
+    e.seed_at([("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("entry".into())), ("bucket", Value::Int(3))]))], 0).unwrap();
+    let digest = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(3)]);
+    let legacy = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(3)]);
+    let target = e.schema().tables["totals"].clone();
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    let mut row = tx.get("totals", &digest).unwrap().unwrap();
+    tx.delete("totals", &target, &row).unwrap();
+    if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row {
+        fields.insert("id".into(), Value::Id(legacy.clone()));
+    }
+    tx.put("totals", &target, &row, None).unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &legacy, 5).unwrap();
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+
+    let removed = compile(WITHOUT_AGGREGATE);
+    e.adopt_at(removed.schema, removed.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &legacy).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest).unwrap(), 5);
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(e.store().meta(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META).unwrap().as_deref(), Some(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION));
+
+    let restored = compile(SOURCE);
+    e.adopt_at(restored.schema, restored.programs, 2).unwrap();
+    e.seed_at([("sequenced".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("next".into())), ("total", Value::Id(digest))]))], 3).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("sequenced").unwrap()[0].field("ordinal"), Ok(&Value::Int(6)));
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn counter_only_aggregate_reference_chains_rekey_to_the_final_digest() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table aEntries:\n  key: int\n  public\nview aTotals: count aEntries by .key\n  public\ntable bEntries:\n  parent: aTotals\n  public\nview bTotals: count bEntries by .parent\n  public\ntable sequenced:\n  parent: bTotals\n  ordinal: sequence by parent\n  public\n";
+    let mut e = open(SOURCE);
+    let legacy_a = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(5)]);
+    let legacy_b = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Id(legacy_a.clone())]);
+    let digest_a = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(5)]);
+    let digest_b = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Id(digest_a)]);
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("bTotals", &legacy_b, 9).unwrap();
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+
+    let next = compile(SOURCE);
+    e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    let tx = e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("bTotals", &legacy_b).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("bTotals", &digest_b).unwrap(), 9, "the absent referenced aggregate identity must settle before its dependent counter key");
+    tx.rollback().unwrap();
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn sequence_identity_rekey_rolls_back_with_the_adoption_writer() {
+    let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
+    let legacy = crate::value::canonical_json(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let digest = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Int(7)]);
+    let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
+    tx.set_sequence("totals", &legacy, 11).unwrap();
+    tx.commit().unwrap();
+
+    let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
+    tx.rewrite_sequence_ids(&BTreeMap::from([(
+        "totals".into(),
+        BTreeMap::from([(legacy.clone(), digest.clone())]),
+    )])).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &legacy).unwrap(), 0);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest).unwrap(), 11);
+    tx.rollback().unwrap();
+
+    let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &legacy).unwrap(), 11);
+    assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("totals", &digest).unwrap(), 0);
+    tx.rollback().unwrap();
+}
+

     rebuild_groups(&GROUPS.replace("count entries by", "count entries where .n < 0 by"), 0);

--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/schema.rs

@@ -1740,12 +1740,27 @@ pub fn maintained_key(source: &Value, by: &[String]) -> Vec<Value> {

-/// The id of a maintained row is its group's canonical form, as Snapback 2
-/// spelled it: an aggregate's identity is its key, and the conformance
-/// corpus holds it to this spelling. Programs read aggregates by group,
-/// never by id.
+/// Activation marker for the maintained-row address function. A missing or
+/// older marker rebuilds every aggregate target before the generation opens.
+pub const MAINTAINED_ID_META: &str = "maintained_id_version";
+pub const MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION: &str = "sha256-v1";
+
+/// The id of a maintained row is a fixed-size digest of its typed group key.
+/// Programs address aggregates by their group index, never by this internal
+/// id. Domain separation makes future encodings explicit migrations.
+// @ref LLP 3000.007.003#30-aggregate-target-identity-and-migration — every aggregate writer, synthetic check row and adoption rebuild shares this bounded identity.
 pub fn maintained_id(key: &[Value]) -> String {
-    crate::value::canonical_json(&key)
+    crate::value::digest(&("snapback4-maintained-id/sha256-v1", key))
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn maintained_identity_is_fixed_size_and_domain_separated() {
+    let short = maintained_id(&[Value::String("x".into())]);
+    let long = maintained_id(&[Value::String("x".repeat(100_000))]);
+    assert_eq!(short.len(), 64);
+    assert_eq!(long.len(), 64);
+    assert_ne!(short, long);
+    assert_ne!(short, crate::value::digest(&[Value::String("x".into())]));
 }

+
+#[test]
+fn missing_identity_marker_rebuilds_a_principal_reference_and_max_text_group() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table parents:\n  public\ntable entries:\n  owner: principal\n  parent: parents\n  token: text <=64\n  amount: int\n  public\nview totals: sum entries of .amount by .owner, .parent, .token\n  public\n";
+    let initial = compile(SOURCE);
+    let mut e = Engine::new_at(Store::memory().unwrap(), initial.schema, initial.programs, 0).unwrap();
+    let owner = format!("user:{}", "o".repeat(59));
+    let parent = "p".repeat(64);
+    let token = "t".repeat(64);
+    assert_eq!((owner.len(), parent.len(), token.len()), (64, 64, 64));
+    e.seed_at([
+        ("parents".into(), object([("id", Value::Id(parent.clone()))])),
+        ("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("entry".into())), ("owner", Value::Id(owner.clone())),
+            ("parent", Value::Id(parent.clone())), ("token", Value::String(token.clone())), ("amount", Value::Int(7))])),
+    ], 0).unwrap();
+    let expected = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[Value::Id(owner), Value::Id(parent), Value::String(token)]);
+    let before = e.store().rows("totals").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(before.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected.clone()));
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(7));
+    assert_eq!(expected.len(), 64);
+
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+    let next = compile(SOURCE);
+    assert_eq!(preview(&mut e, &next.schema).aggregate_rows, 1);
+    let generation = e.generation();
+    e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    assert!(e.generation() > generation);
+    assert_eq!(e.store().meta(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META).unwrap().as_deref(), Some(crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_VERSION));
+    let after = e.store().rows("totals").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(after.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected));
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(7));
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn identity_redeployment_rebuilds_the_ungrouped_empty_tuple() {
+    const SOURCE: &str = "table entries:\n  public\nview total: count entries\n  public\n";
+    let initial = compile(SOURCE);
+    let mut e = Engine::new_at(Store::memory().unwrap(), initial.schema, initial.programs, 0).unwrap();
+    e.seed_at([("entries".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("entry".into()))]))], 0).unwrap();
+    let expected = crate::schema::maintained_id(&[]);
+    let before = e.store().rows("total").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(before.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected.clone()));
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("$global").unwrap(), &Value::Bool(true));
+    assert_eq!(before[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(1));
+
+    e.store().conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [crate::schema::MAINTAINED_ID_META]).unwrap();
+    let next = compile(SOURCE);
+    assert_eq!(preview(&mut e, &next.schema).aggregate_rows, 1);
+    let generation = e.generation();
+    e.adopt_at(next.schema, next.programs, 1).unwrap();
+    assert!(e.generation() > generation);
+    let after = e.store().rows("total").unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(after.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id(expected));
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("$global").unwrap(), &Value::Bool(true));
+    assert_eq!(after[0].field("value").unwrap(), &Value::Int(1));
+}
+

 fn changed_filter_counts_cleared_retained_view_rows() {
     rebuild_groups(&GROUPS.replace("count entries by", "count entries where .n < 0 by"), 0);

    78	    /// Steps 3–6. The walk holds identities, never images: each child scan
    79	    /// streams `PAGE` rows per seek, and every write rereads its row current.
    80	    fn reference_closure(&mut self, root_table: &str, root: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    81	        let schema = self.schema;
    82	        let mut seen: BTreeSet<(String, String)> = BTreeSet::from([(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned())]);

    84	        let mut visits: Vec<(String, Value)> = vec![(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.clone())];

   119	            let via = if parent == root_table { String::new() } else { format!(", through a {parent} row this delete cascades to") };
   120	            return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_REFERENCED", message: format!("{root_table} at {} is still referenced through {child}.{column} (on delete restrict){via}; delete or retarget those rows first, or declare the reference's action (guide writes)", self.statement_site) });
   121	        }
   122	        // A cascaded row can itself have a physically retained read-through
   123	        // child. Decide that blocker before set-null writes begin, so expiry
   124	        // can retain this root and continue without relying on transaction
   125	        // rollback to undo a partial closure.
   126	        for (table, id) in visits.iter().skip(1) {
   127	            if self.physical_children_outside(table, id, &seen)? {
   128	                return Err(self.parent_has_children(table));
   129	            }
   130	        }
   131	        // 5. Set null outside the closure. Each group is gathered whole before
   132	        // any of it is cleared: a clear can recount a sibling that shares the
   133	        // reference's index and move it behind a page cursor. Each row is then
   134	        // reread current before its write.
   135	        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — gather, then clear: no row moves out of a walk it has not finished.
   136	        for (table, id) in &visits {
   137	            for r in schema.referencing(table) {
   138	                if r.action != OnDelete::SetNull { continue; }
   139	                let mut group: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   140	                self.each_child(&r, id, true, &mut |_, child| {
   141	                    let child = child.as_str()?.to_owned();
   142	                    if !seen.contains(&(r.child.to_owned(), child.clone())) { group.push(child); }
   143	                    Ok(true)
   144	                })?;
   145	                for child in group {
   146	                    let current = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, r.child, &child))?;
   147	                    if let Some(current) = current.filter(|row| row.field(r.column).is_ok_and(|v| v == id)) {
   148	                        self.clear_reference(r.child, r.column, current)?;
   149	                    }
   150	                }
   151	            }
   152	        }
   153	        // 6. Delete the closure, children first, each from its current image.
   154	        let reached: Vec<(String, String)> = visits[1..].iter().map(|(t, id)| Ok((t.clone(), id.as_str()?.to_owned()))).collect::<Result<_, RuntimeError>>()?;
   155	        drop(visits);
   156	        for (table, id) in deletion_order(schema, &reached) {
   157	            let current = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, &table, &id))?;
   158	            let Some(current) = current else { continue };
   159	            self.delete_reached(&table, current)?;
   160	        }
   161	        Ok(())
   162	    }
   163	
   164	    /// Streams the ids of the live rows referencing `id` through `r` to
   165	    /// `visit`, through its index, until `visit` answers false. A capped walk
   166	    /// (cascade, set null) reads `PAGE` rows per seek and holds at most the
   167	    /// reference's cap; a restrict probe reads one row per seek, so it examines
   168	    /// nothing past its witness. Images are dropped before `visit` runs. Seeks
   169	    /// start at the live suffix, so an expired backlog is never visited.
   170	    fn each_child(&mut self, r: &Referencing<'_>, id: &Value, capped: bool, visit: &mut dyn FnMut(&mut Self, Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   171	        let schema = self.schema;
   172	        let index = schema.reference_index(r.child, r.column).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} has no reference index", r.child, r.column)))?.to_owned();
   173	        let (cap, page_rows) = if capped { (schema.reference_cap(r.child, r.column).map_or(u64::MAX, u64::from), PAGE) } else { (u64::MAX, 1) };
   174	        // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.3 — seek on the effective deadline (a read-through child's `$deadline`), so no expired row is visited.
   175	        let ranges = if r.table.expiry_column().is_some() && !through_reference(r) {
   176	            let now = self.context.now.ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("delete clock is not pinned".into()))?;
   177	            vec![(vec![id.clone(), Value::Null], None), (vec![id.clone()], Some(Value::Int(now)))]
   178	        } else {
   179	            vec![(vec![id.clone()], None)]
   180	        };
   181	        let mut live = 0_u64;
   182	        for (prefix, gt) in ranges {
   183	            let mut bounds = Bounds { gt, ..Default::default() };
   184	            loop {
   185	                let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.scan(allowance, r.child, &index, &prefix, &bounds, Dir::Asc, page_rows))?;
   186	                let more = page.len() == page_rows;
   187	                if let Some((_, key)) = page.last() { bounds.after = crate::key::decode(key).map(|v| v.into_iter().skip(prefix.len()).collect()); }
   188	                let mut ids = Vec::with_capacity(page.len());
   189	                for (row, _) in page {
   190	                    if self.device {
   191	                        // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — a device predicts only over current rows: retained history queues the delete, and only a live, current (or own provisional) row is a witness.
   192	                        // A retained, no longer current row is work the reservation does not
   193	                        // price and a witness the device cannot judge: the delete queues.
   194	                        if self.tx.retained_row(r.child, &row)? && !self.tx.current_row(r.child, &row)? {
   195	                            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting meets retained {} history that references it; queued without a local delete", r.child)));
   196	                        }
   197	                        if self.expired(r.table, &row) || !(self.tx.current_row(r.child, &row)? || self.is_provisional(r.child, &row)?) {
   198	                            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting needs a current image of every {} row that references it; queued without a local delete", r.child)));
   199	                        }
   200	                    } else if self.expired(r.table, &row) { continue; }
   201	                    live += 1;
   202	                    // Activation validates caps and every write keeps them; more than the cap
   203	                    // is a store that broke its own invariant, never a hidden-row count.
   204	                    if live > cap { return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} holds more rows than its cap for one {} row", r.child, r.column, r.column))); }
   205	                    ids.push(row.field("id")?.clone());
   206	                }
   207	                for child in ids {
   208	                    if !visit(self, child)? { return Ok(()); }
   209	                }
   210	                if !more { break; }
   211	            }
   212	        }
   213	        Ok(())
   214	    }
   215	
   216	    /// A device predicts only over a referencing group it holds completely.
   217	    // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — the held-group proof of LLP 3000.007 §20.5, per closure row and reference.
   218	    fn prove_reference_held(&mut self, r: &Referencing<'_>, id: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   219	        let index = self.schema.reference_index(r.child, r.column).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} has no reference index", r.child, r.column)))?.to_owned();
   220	        let (child, key) = (r.child.to_owned(), [id.clone()]);
   221	        // Key-decided readability, and the group itself held (a rejoined or
   222	        // unacquired range is unknown even when the rule admits the viewer).
   223	        if self.device_work(|runner| runner.upsert_absence_proven(&child, &index, &key))? && self.tx.current_group(&child, &index, &key)? { return Ok(()); }
   224	        Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting needs every {} row that references it held; queued without a local delete", r.child)))
   225	    }
   226	
   227	    /// The engine's own write of a `set null` reference: no rule, every constraint.
   228	    fn clear_reference(&mut self, table: &str, column: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   229	        let mut new = old.clone();
   230	        if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut new { fields.insert(column.to_owned(), Value::Null); }
   231	        let patch = crate::value::object([(column, Value::Null)]);
   232	        let outer = std::mem::replace(&mut self.engine_action, true);
   233	        let result = self.target_attempt(table, |runner| {
   234	            runner.reserve_maintains(table, &runner.schema.tables[table])?;
   235	            runner.replace_authorized(table, old, new, true, &patch, 0)
   236	        });
   237	        self.engine_action = outer;
   238	        result
   239	    }
   240	
   241	    /// The engine's own delete of a closure row: no rule, its constraints, its
   242	    /// `read through` children's probe, maintains and partition events.
   243	    fn delete_reached(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   244	        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
   245	            runner.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Delete)?;
   246	            let table = &runner.schema.tables[table_name];
   247	            runner.reserve_maintains(table_name, table)?;
   248	            runner.delete_row(table_name, old)
   249	        })
   250	    }
   251	}
   252	
   253	/// Rows a child scan holds per seek: the reservation's transient `4 × row`
   254	/// (LLP 3000.039 §5.1, `Schema::delete_reservation`).
   255	const PAGE: usize = crate::schema::references::REFERENCE_PAGE;
   256	
   257	#[cfg(test)]
   258	mod tests {
   259	    use super::*;
   260	    use crate::{Context, rows::{MemoryRows, Rows}, value::{Value, object}};
   261	
   262	    #[test]
   263	    fn prepaid_actions_keep_nested_device_proofs_bounded() {
   264	        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("nested-allowance.q".into(), r#"
   265	table parents:
   266	  public
   267	table children:
   268	  parent: parents on delete cascade
   269	  cap 1 by parent
   270	  public
   271	"#.into())]);
   272	        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
   273	        let schema: crate::Schema = crate::json_codec::from_text(
   274	            &crate::json_codec::to_text(&bound.schema).unwrap(),
   275	        ).unwrap();
   276	        let rows = MemoryRows::new();
   277	        let parent = object([("id", Value::Id("p".into()))]);
   278	        rows.put("parents", &schema.tables["parents"], &parent, None).unwrap();
   279	        rows.put("children", &schema.tables["children"], &object([
   280	            ("id", Value::Id("c".into())), ("parent", Value::Id("p".into())),
   281	        ]), None).unwrap();
   282	        let price = schema.delete_reservation("parents").unwrap();
   283	        let ceilings = Ceilings {
   284	            steps: price[0], index_entries: price[1], examined: price[2],
   285	            rows_written: price[3], heap_bytes: price[4], output_bytes: price[5],
   286	            rule_probes: price[6],
   287	        };
   288	        let mut runner = Runner::new(&rows, &schema, Context::system().at(1), Meter::new(ceilings, Cut::Abort), 0);
   289	        runner.device = true;
   290	        // Model the commit-end walk after its outer device_work swap: the
   291	        // bounded allowance is the active meter and no spare copy exists.
   292	        runner.device_work_active = true;
   293	        let error = runner.apply_reference_actions("parents", &parent).unwrap_err();
   294	        assert!(matches!(&error, RuntimeError::Prediction(message) if message.contains("allowance exhausted")), "{error:?}");
   295	        assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.steps, price[0] + 1, "the nested proof attempted its bounded step");
   296	    }
   297	}
   298	
   299	fn through_reference(r: &Referencing<'_>) -> bool {
   300	    r.table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == r.column)
   301	}
   302	
   303	/// The closure's rows, children before parents: a table goes first when no
   304	/// other reached table must be removed before it (the cascade and `read
   305	/// through` edges are acyclic by `check`), and within a table, later
   306	/// discoveries first.
   307	fn deletion_order(schema: &crate::schema::Schema, reached: &[(String, String)]) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
   308	    let tables: Vec<&str> = {
   309	        let mut seen = Vec::new();
   310	        for (table, _) in reached { if !seen.contains(&table.as_str()) { seen.push(table.as_str()); } }
   311	        seen
   312	    };
   313	    // edges: child table -> the reached tables it must be removed before
   314	    // (its cascade parents and its `read through` parent; LLP 3000.039 §3).
   315	    let mut edges: Vec<(&str, &str)> = Vec::new();
   316	    for &child in &tables {
   317	        for &parent in &tables {
   318	            if child != parent && schema.deletion_precedes(child, parent) { edges.push((child, parent)); }
   319	        }
   320	    }
   321	    let mut order: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
   322	    let mut remaining = tables.clone();
   323	    while !remaining.is_empty() {
   324	        // A table no remaining table references can go now (its referencers are gone).
   325	        let position = remaining.iter().position(|t| !remaining.iter().any(|other| other != t && edges.contains(&(*other, *t))))
   326	            .unwrap_or(remaining.len() - 1);
   327	        order.push(remaining.remove(position));
   328	    }
   329	    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(reached.len());
   330	    for table in order {
   331	        out.extend(reached.iter().rev().filter(|(t, _)| t == table).cloned());
   332	    }
   333	    out
   334	}

   336	/// Trusted restoration validates the existing immutable captures; it never stamps them.
   337	pub fn validate_restore(tx: &dyn Rows, schema: &Schema, now: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   338	    for name in schema.through_order() {
   339	        let table = &schema.tables[&name];
   340	        let Some(t) = &table.through else {
   341	            continue;
   342	        };
   343	        let parent = &schema.tables[&t.parent];
   344	        let mut invalid = 0;
   345	        let mut samples = Vec::new();
   346	        rows(tx, &name, |row| {
   347	            let found = row
   348	                .field(&t.reference)
   349	                .ok()
   350	                .and_then(|v| v.as_str().ok())
   351	                .map(|id| tx.get(&t.parent, id))
   352	                .transpose()?
   353	                .flatten();
   354	            let matches = found.as_ref().is_some_and(|p| {
   355	                (!parent.expired(p, now) || table.expired(&row, now)) && {
   356	                    let p = crate::schema::row_image(p, parent);
   357	                    t.fields
   358	                        .iter()
   359	                        .all(|(field, source)| row.field(field).ok() == p.field(source).ok())
   360	                        && row.field(PARENT_DEADLINE).ok()
   361	                            == Some(&parent.deadline(&p).map(Value::Int).unwrap_or(Value::Null))
   362	                }
   363	            });
   364	            if !matches {
   365	                invalid += 1;
   366	                sample(&mut samples, &row, &t.reference, &t.parent);
   367	            }
   368	            Ok(())
   369	        })?;
   370	        if invalid > 0 {
   371	            return Err(refusal(
   372	                &name,
   373	                invalid,
   374	                "restored captures do not match a live parent",
   375	                &samples,
   376	            ));
   377	        }
   378	    }
   379	    Ok(())
   380	}
   381	
   382	pub fn validate_indexes(tx: &dyn Rows, schema: &Schema) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   383	    for (name, table) in &schema.tables {
   384	        let mut checks: Vec<_> = table
   385	            .indexes
   386	            .iter()
   387	            .filter(|(_, i)| i.unique)
   388	            .map(|(name, i)| (name, i.components.len(), 1, true))
   389	            .collect();
   390	        for constraint in &table.constraints {
   391	            if let crate::schema::Constraint::Cap { by, max } = constraint {
   392	                let (index, _) = table
   393	                    .indexes
   394	                    .iter()
   395	                    .find(|(_, i)| i.columns().starts_with(by))
   396	                    .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{name} cap has no index")))?;
   397	                checks.push((index, by.len(), u64::from(*max), false));
   398	            }
   399	        }
   400	        for (index, width, max, nulls_distinct) in checks {
   401	            let mut bounds = Bounds::default();
   402	            let mut previous = None;
   403	            let mut count = 0;
   404	            loop {
   405	                let page = tx.scan(name, index, &[], &bounds, Dir::Asc, 256)?;
   406	                if page.is_empty() {
   407	                    break;
   408	                }
   409	                for (_, key) in page {
   410	                    let full = crate::key::decode(&key)
   411	                        .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Store("invalid activation index".into()))?;
   412	                    let group = full[..width].to_vec();
   413	                    bounds.after = Some(full);
   414	                    if nulls_distinct && group.contains(&Value::Null) {
   415	                        continue;
   416	                    }
   417	                    if previous.as_ref() == Some(&group) {
   418	                        count += 1;
   419	                    } else {
   420	                        previous = Some(group);
   421	                        count = 1;
   422	                    }
   423	                    if count > max {
   424	                        return Err(RuntimeError::Constraint(format!(
   425	                            "cannot activate {name}.{index}: existing group exceeds {max}"
   426	                        )));
   427	                    }
   428	                }
   429	            }
   430	        }
   431	    }
   432	    Ok(())
   433	}
