   118	
   119	## 2. What exists and what does not
   120	
   121	| Form | Today | This RFC |
   122	|---|---|---|
   123	| `count` or `sum` by the source's own columns, filtered, with `day`, `hour` or `bucket` keys | Built (§20.9) | Unchanged; one instance of the substrate |
   124	| F1, maintained column | Accepted (LLP 3000.007.001); stopped at MC1's gate 5 | After the §5 ruling and prerequisite |
   125	| F2, attribution through a reference | Refused ("no automatic join") | Engine-recorded, over public targets |
   126	| F3, timeline | Application recipe (`init --recipe feeds`) | The recipe's contract as a declaration, in the recipe's shape |
   127	
   128	## 3. The forms
   129	
   130	Spellings are illustrative. None of the blocks below is a compiling example.
   131	
   132	### 3.0 Aggregate target identity and migration
   133	
   134	Every ordinary maintained aggregate row has one internal identity function:
   135	64 lowercase hexadecimal characters from SHA-256 over the domain
   136	`snapback4-maintained-id/sha256-v1` and the typed canonical group-key vector.
   137	The checker therefore admits the largest legal group key against a fixed-size
   138	identity, and synthetic check rows, incremental writes, point reads and
   139	adoption rebuilds all call the same function. The group key remains the public
   140	address; programs do not read or supply this internal id.
   141	
   142	The intrinsic `byId` order of aggregate rows is consequently opaque digest
   143	order. It is stable for one identity version but has no relationship to the
   144	lexical or numeric order of group fields, and migration may change it. A query
   145	whose result needs group-key order declares that order explicitly (for example,
   146	`first 50 by .emoji`) rather than relying on an unqualified aggregate scan.
   147	
   148	The SQLite metadata and evaluation-PostgreSQL backend marker
   149	`maintained_id_version = sha256-v1` bind persisted aggregate targets to that
   150	function. Opening or adopting either store without the marker — including a
   151	0.4.28 store whose ids were the unbounded canonical JSON of the group key —
   152	atomically clears and rebuilds every ordinary aggregate target from its source
   153	rows, writes the marker, and publishes a new semantic generation. A failure
   154	rolls back old rows, rebuilt rows, marker and generation together. Adoption
   155	advice counts the SQLite rebuild even when schema/program bytes are unchanged;
   156	the evaluation adapter performs the corresponding rebuild under its opening
   157	writer/head fence. Its narrowly admitted private maintained-column targets use
   158	the same digest migration: rebuild the authoritative target and then mirror
   159	every live parent before the marker or generation publishes. Before clearing
   160	targets, adoption derives each target's
   161	legacy-id-to-digest map to a fixed point over typed reference dependencies,
   162	then rewrites every surviving typed reference through those maps, including
   163	rows, columns and narrowed rows retained in release-dormant storage. Its
   164	captured dormant-reference ledger changes in the same transaction, so a later
   165	restore still proves the same aggregate lifetime under its digest address. Thus an
   166	aggregate grouped by a reference to another aggregate uses that referenced
   167	aggregate's new digest when deriving its own digest, and a table that refers
   168	to either rebuilt target does not dangle. A legacy id with no matching stored
   169	target stays opaque, preserving the existing dangling-reference contract.
   170	Removing the aggregate itself retires its derived target rows; a dormant
   171	reference to that retired target receives no map and keeps the ordinary
   172	restore refusal against acquiring a newly created parent lifetime.
   173	
   174	Persisted `sequence by` custody is part of the same identity migration even
   175	when no allocating row, source row, or aggregate target row survives. Adoption
   176	decodes canonical legacy counter ids with the predecessor aggregate's typed
   177	group definition, recursively settles any aggregate-reference components, and
   178	re-keys `_sequences(parent, id, value)` through the resulting target-specific
   179	map. If legacy and digest keys both exist, their maximum high-water value wins;
   180	the legacy key is removed and an exhausted counter remains exhausted. Counters
   181	for other parent tables and legacy strings that do not decode as a canonical
   182	group tuple stay opaque. A predecessor aggregate removed by the candidate is
   183	normalized before the current identity marker publishes, so later
   184	reintroduction cannot revive legacy custody under that marker. Counter rewrites
   185	and their rollback share the adoption transaction with row-reference rewrites,
   186	target rebuilds, marker and generation.
   187	
   188	Reference rewrites, index updates, target rebuilds, marker and generation share
   189	the same transaction. A legacy target address is itself the predecessor's
   190	canonical logical group tuple. Adoption decodes it using the predecessor
   191	maintain and column types, so candidate projection cannot erase the migration
   192	input when the same activation also changes the grouping declaration. The map
   193	preserves the predecessor target identity; the candidate rebuild then decides
   194	which new groups exist, so a reference to a removed group follows the ordinary
   195	dangling-reference contract. The fixed-point bound counts stored aggregate identities,
   196	not aggregate declarations, because one target can hold a legal acyclic chain
   197	of references to other rows in that same target. SQLite captures newly recorded
   198	path values while predecessor targets still exist, then derives and applies all
   199	legacy maps before any recorded-path rebuild can remove those targets. Schemas
   200	with ordinary maintains require device capability
   201	`maintained-id-sha256-v1`; an older device is refused at the handshake before
   202	it can compute a legacy id and silently read a digest-addressed aggregate as
   203	zero. Conversely, a current device refuses to execute backend bytes from an
   204	old server that omit this capability. The shared codec records the omission so
   205	the server can adopt and republish a persisted schema; a device may decode the
   206	same bytes only as an inactive kept predecessor that it atomically replaces
   207	with a current backend, preserving queued writes. Offline reuse and adoption
   208	of the old backend refuse before interpretation.
   209	
   210	### 3.1 F1: maintained columns
   440	
   441	    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   442	    fn rebuild_maintain_inner(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   443	        if maintain.row_key.is_some() { return self.rebuild_row_view(maintain); }
   444	        let target = self.schema.table(&maintain.target).expect("validated");
   445	        let mut bounds = Bounds::default();
   446	        let target_bytes = super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(target).saturating_mul(16 + 4 * target.indexes.len() as u64);
   447	        self.meter.heap(target_bytes.saturating_mul(256))?;
   448	        loop {
   449	            let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.scan(allowance, &maintain.target, "byId", &[], &bounds, Dir::Asc, 256))?;
   450	            if page.is_empty() { break; }
   451	            for (existing, key) in page {
   452	                bounds.after = crate::key::decode(&key);
   453	                self.refuse_timeline_write(&maintain.target, crate::schema::Effect::Delete)?;
   454	                let entries = self.tx.delete(&maintain.target, target, &existing)?;
   455	                self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::IndexEntries, entries)?;
   456	                self.row_changed(&maintain.target, Some(&existing), None, Origin::Maintained)?;
   457	            }
   458	        }
   459	        let source = self.schema.table(&maintain.source).expect("validated");
   460	        self.meter.heap(super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(source).saturating_mul(256))?;
   461	        let mut totals: BTreeMap<String, (Vec<Value>, i128)> = BTreeMap::new();
   462	        bounds = Bounds::default();
   463	        loop {
   464	            let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.scan(allowance, &maintain.source, "byId", &[], &bounds, Dir::Asc, 256))?;
   465	            if page.is_empty() { break; }
   466	            for (mut image, key) in page {
   467	                self.rebuilt_source_rows += 1;
   468	                bounds.after = crate::key::decode(&key);
   469	                materialize_optional_fields(&mut image, source);
   470	                self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
   471	                self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::Steps, maintain.evaluation_steps())?;
   472	                if !maintain.selected(&image) { continue; }
   473	                // @ref LLP 3000.028#6 — a total unique owner key has one contribution; backfill streams bounded pages instead of retaining all lifetime keys in a totals map.
   474	                if maintain.own_keys.is_some() {
   475	                    let heap = self.meter.used.heap_bytes;
   476	                    self.meter.heap(target_bytes)?;
   477	                    self.apply_aggregate(maintain, maintain.source_key(&image), 1, Some(true))?;
   478	                    self.meter.used.heap_bytes = heap;
   479	                    continue;
   480	                }
   481	                self.meter.heap(target_bytes)?;
   482	                let key = maintain.source_key(&image);
   483	                let change = delta(maintain, &image, 1)?;
   484	                let entry = match totals.entry(canonical_json(&key)) {
   485	                    std::collections::btree_map::Entry::Occupied(entry) => {
   486	                        self.meter.used.heap_bytes -= target_bytes;
   487	                        entry.into_mut()
   488	                    }
   489	                    std::collections::btree_map::Entry::Vacant(entry) => entry.insert((key, 0)),
   490	                };
   491	                entry.1 = entry.1.checked_add(change).ok_or_else(|| aggregate_overflow(maintain))?;
   492	            }
   493	        }
   494	        for (_, (key, change)) in totals {
   495	            if change != 0 || self.live_sum(maintain) {
   496	                self.apply_aggregate(maintain, key, change, Some(true))?;
   497	            }
   498	        }
   499	        Ok(())
   500	    }
   501	
   502	    fn live_sum(&self, maintain: &Maintain) -> bool {
   503	        matches!(maintain.kind, MaintainKind::Sum { .. }) && self.schema.table(&maintain.source).is_some_and(|source| source.is_expiring())
   504	    }
   505	
   506	    /// A zero sum is still a group while source rows remain: one signed
   507	    /// contribution may expire before the other. Keep its candidate row so
   508	    /// a scan, snapshot and offline replica can discover the later live sum.
   509	    fn aggregate_source_present(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain, key: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
   510	        // One returned source image and its encoded key (MemoryRows also materializes count_prefix).
   511	        let bytes = super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[&maintain.source]).saturating_mul(2);
   512	        self.maintain_scratch(bytes, |runner| {
   513	            let source = &runner.schema.tables[&maintain.source];
   514	            let index = maintain.contribution_index();
   515	            if !source.indexes.contains_key(&index) { return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("maintain {} needs its contribution index", maintain.target))); }
   516	            runner.charge_maintain(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
   517	            let present = runner.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.count_prefix(allowance, &maintain.source, &index, key, 1))? != 0;
   518	            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9 — a local positive witness keeps zero; absence needs complete, currently held source coverage.
   519	            if !present && runner.device && !runner.aggregate_source_complete(maintain, key)? {
   520	                runner.unknown_fact(&maintain.source, &index, key, "zero-sum occupancy");
   521	                return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction("the zero-sum group's source occupancy is not known locally; queued without a predicted row".into()));
   522	            }
   523	            Ok(present)
   524	        })
   525	    }
   526	
   527	    fn aggregate_source_complete(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain, key: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
   528	        let source = &self.schema.tables[&maintain.source];
   529	        if source.sync.as_ref().is_none_or(|sync| sync.horizon.is_some()) || self.schema.maintain_subset_unknown(&maintain.target) { return Ok(false); }
   530	        // The target has the same bare audience keys, but no source horizon.
   531	        if !self.upsert_absence_proven(&maintain.target, "byGroup", &target_group_key(maintain, key))? { return Ok(false); }
   532	        for (name, index) in &source.indexes {
   533	            let values = index.columns().iter().map_while(|column| maintain.by.iter().position(|field| field == column).map(|n| key[n].clone())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
   534	            if self.retained_group(&maintain.source, name, &values)? { return Ok(false); }
   535	        }
   536	        Ok(true)
   537	    }
   538	
   539	    fn apply_aggregate(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain, key: Vec<Value>, change: i128, source_present: Option<bool>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   540	        if self.schema.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.owns(&maintain.target)) {
   541	            return Err(super::timeline::refusal("E_TIMELINE_OWNED", "timeline-owned tables cannot be aggregate targets; maintain a mapped application table instead"));
   542	        }
   543	        let id = maintained_id(&key);
   544	        let target = self.schema.table(&maintain.target).expect("validated");
   545	        self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
   546	        let existing = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, &maintain.target, &id))?;
   547	        let target_key = target_group_key(maintain, &key);
   548	        if self.device && existing.is_none() && !self.upsert_absence_proven(&maintain.target, "byGroup", &target_key)? {
   549	            self.unknown_fact(&maintain.target, "byGroup", &target_key, "aggregate base");
   550	            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction("the affected aggregate value or its absence is not known locally; queued without a predicted row".into()));
   551	        }
   552	        let old_value = existing.as_ref().and_then(|row| row.field("value").ok()).map(Value::as_int).transpose()?.unwrap_or(0);
   553	        let next = i128::from(old_value) + change;

   555	            return Err(aggregate_overflow(maintain));
   556	        }

   558	            Some(present) => present,
   559	            None => self.aggregate_source_present(maintain, &key)?,
   560	        };
   561	        if next == 0 && !keep_zero {
   562	            if let Some(existing) = existing {
   563	                self.refuse_timeline_write(&maintain.target, crate::schema::Effect::Delete)?;
   564	                let entries = self.tx.delete(&maintain.target, target, &existing)?;
   565	                self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::IndexEntries, entries)?;
   566	                self.row_changed(&maintain.target, Some(&existing), None, Origin::Maintained)?;
   567	            }
   568	            self.mirror_column(maintain, &key, 0)?;
   569	            return Ok(());
   570	        }
   571	        let mut row = crate::small_map::collect([("id".to_owned(), Value::Id(id.clone())), ("value".to_owned(), Value::Int(next as i64))]);

   573	            row.insert(field.clone(), value);
   574	        }

   576	        if maintain.by.is_empty() { row.insert(GLOBAL.into(), Value::Bool(true)); }
   577	        let row = Value::Row(row.into());
   578	        if existing.as_ref() == Some(&row) { return Ok(()); }
   579	        let entries = self.tx.put(&maintain.target, target, &row, existing.as_ref())?;
   580	        self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::IndexEntries, entries)?;
   581	        self.row_changed(&maintain.target, existing.as_ref(), Some(&row), Origin::Maintained)?;
   582	        self.mirror_column(maintain, &key, next as i64)
   583	    }
   584	}

   586	pub(super) fn delta(maintain: &Maintain, source: &Value, direction: i64) -> Result<i128, RuntimeError> {
   587	    Ok(match &maintain.kind {
   588	        MaintainKind::Count => i128::from(direction),
   589	        MaintainKind::Sum { of } => i128::from(direction).checked_mul(i128::from(source.field(of)?.as_int()?)).ok_or_else(|| aggregate_overflow(maintain))?,
   590	    })
   591	}
   592	
   593	// @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — an internal sum names its maintain and column, never a retained contribution or computed value.
   594	pub(super) fn aggregate_overflow(maintain: &Maintain) -> RuntimeError {
   595	    RuntimeError::AggregateOverflow { maintain: maintain.private.as_ref().map_or_else(|| maintain.target.clone(), |o|o.table.clone()), column: maintain.private.as_ref().map_or_else(|| "value".into(), |o|o.column.clone()) }
   596	}

- [maintains.rs:378](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/maintains.rs:378): Counter settlement can override an existing row-identity map with a different digest. Consider aggregate `B` grouped by `Ref<A>`, where `B` survives but the referenced `A` target was deleted. Row migration correctly preserves the dangling legacy `A` reference, so rebuilt `B` and its surviving references use `hash([legacy_A])`. Sequence dependency collection independently decodes the absent `A` identity (lines 80–87), and counter settlement instead maps the same legacy `B` address to `hash([digest_A])`. Its high-water value moves away from the actual rebuilt parent; subsequent allocations restart at 1, reusing ordinals or failing existing uniqueness constraints. Preserve established row mappings when settling counter-only dependencies, and cover this mixed surviving/dangling chain.

Its high-water value moves away from the actual rebuilt parent; subsequent allocations restart at 1, reusing ordinals or failing existing uniqueness constraints.
